# Verification report

Generated by `tools/build_verification_report.py` from actual runs; do not edit by hand. Policy, tiers and tolerances: [VERIFICATION.md](VERIFICATION.md).

> 'verified' means only: the reference solution passed every tier listed as applicable in this record, at the tolerances stated in it. It does not mean reviewed, approved or certified by anyone.

## 1. Summary

Software: benchfea 0.1.0, python 3.13.18, numpy 2.5.3, scipy 1.18.1, foreign:opensees 3.8.0.0, foreign:pynite 3.2.1.

* Problem library: 173 problem(s) — 109 passed, 0 failed, 64 quarantined, 0 invalid.
* Of the 109 passed problem(s), 74 have a model whose record is `verified` (every applicable tier passed, including the cross-check by two foreign solvers).
* Model bank: 50 model(s) — 36 verified, 13 quarantined, 1 refused by the engine.
* Run time of this report: 211 s (single BLAS thread).

### 1.1 What the problems compare against

A problem that *passed* is not always a reproduction of a published number. Bases: `published` = every target is a number printed in the source; `recomputed-from-published-statement` = the problem STATEMENT is published, the targets were computed in this repository by means that share no code with the engine (ODE oracle, a separate small frame program, closed-form expressions evaluated here) -- evidence of the same kind as tier A, two computations made by this project -- plus those printed values of the source that reproduce; `derived-here` = tier A. Printed values of such a source that do NOT reproduce are kept as separate problems `<id>-vendor-printed`, quarantined. Targets *by construction* are printed numbers the model was calibrated on (no evidence).

| source | basis | problems | passed | quarantined | failed | targets of the passed problems: printed in the source | by construction | recomputed here | derived here |

| ansys | published | 2 | 0 | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

| derived | derived-here | 17 | 17 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 101 |

## 2. Problem library (`verification/problems`)

173 problem(s): **109 passed**, **0 failed**, **64 quarantined**, **0 invalid**.

Columns: *outcome* of the problem (targets + tiers that show a disagreement); *largest* = largest relative difference between a target and the engine; *model record* = status of `verify_model` on the problem's model (a model is `verified` only if every applicable tier passed; a model that fewer than two foreign solvers can represent is `quarantined (not-representable)`: it is not cross-checked and not publishable as verified, although its targets are reproduced).

### 2.1 Targets and tiers, problem by problem

#### `aisc-commentary/case-1-si-with-shear-p0` — AISC benchmark Case 1 (P-delta only), SI table, with shear deformation, P = 0

Outcome: **passed** (declared `active`). Source: ANSI/AISC 360-22, Specification for Structural Steel Buildings (American Institute of Steel Construction), Commentary; edition: August 1, 2022; location: Commentary Section C2.1, Fig. C-C2.2 (benchmark problem Case 1), SI table, column P = 0, unbracketed values; pages: p. 16.1-337 (text p. 16.1-336 to 16.1-337). Basis: published.

| target | expected | engine | difference | relative | tolerance | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `memberStation(member=m, quantity=Mz, relative=0.5)` | -26600000 N mm | -26557728.5 | 4.23e+04 | 1.59e-03 | rel 0.03 | ok |
| `memberStation(member=m, quantity=uy, relative=0.5)` | 5.13 mm | 5.13688237 | 6.88e-03 | 1.34e-03 | rel 0.05 | ok |

* tier A (closed form / ODE oracle): passed — 1 combination(s) against the ODE oracle: agree Largest: rotation: 4.62e-16 (tol. 1.00e-06); translation: 3.46e-16 (tol. 1.00e-06).
* tier B (published problem): passed — 2 target(s) of problem 'case-1-si-with-shear-p0' [published]: all reproduced Largest: memberStation(member=m, quantity=Mz, relative=0.5): 1.59e-03 (tol. rel 0.03); memberStation(member=m, quantity=uy, relative=0.5): 1.34e-03 (tol. rel 0.05).
* tier C (independent solvers): NOT passed (not-representable) — not passed (C: only 0 solver(s) can represent: opensees: cannot represent the model: shearDeformation (OpenSees 3.8 ElasticTimoshenkoBeam ignores the geometric nonlinearity of the transformation (measured); the member is modelled WITHOUT shear deformation); pynite: cannot represent the model: shearDeformation (PyNite members are Euler-Bernoulli (no shear area input); the member is modelled WITHOUT shear deformation))
* tier D (invariants): passed — 13 invariant check(s) over 1 combination(s) and the statics of 3 result station(s): all passed Largest: station integration (deflection uy, member m at x = 4265 mm): 1.90e-15 (tol. 1.00e-06); member compatibility: 6.93e-16 (tol. 1.00e-06).
* tier E (equivalence checks): passed — 2 check(s) applied (designVariables, memberSplit); all passed Largest: memberSplit: 7.89e-14 (tol. 1.00e-06); designVariables: 0.00e+00 (tol. 0.00e+00).

* limits: Section properties are calibrated on the source's own P = 0 values (the source gives none): the problem verifies the second-order amplification, not section properties. It does NOT test shear deformation: the source's acceptance tolerance (3 % on moments, 5 % on deflections) is wider than the whole effect of shear deformation in its tables (at most 1.3 % on a moment and 3.5 % on a deflection between the bracketed and unbracketed values), so these targets would also be met without shear deformation; and the shear stiffness is calibrated on a difference the source prints to one significant digit.

* limits: The source does not state the beam area, the leaning column / link properties or the treatment of axial and shear deformation in its software; see README.md for the measured influence of the choices made here.
* **quarantine diagnosis**: 5 of 7 targets missed (drift, base shears, and column moments where printed with three figures). Cause: the source's model is not completely specified, and the missing part matters at the printed precision. The source's figures are output of unnamed analysis software; the source gives Ag and Ix of the W12x65 and Ix of the W18x40 but no shear areas, and does not say whether shear deformation is included. The model built here from the given data (no shear deformation) is systematically stiffer: first-order drift 1.8 % to 2.2 % smaller, base shears and column moments 0.4 % to 1.0 % larger than printed, vertical reactions reproduced (they follow from statics). Evidence that shear deformation in the source's software is the cause (parameter study, README.md; Example C.1B, first order, LRFD: printed drift 0.145 in, base shears 5.64 and 6.21 kips; without shear deformation 0.1422 in, 5.671 and 6.247 kips): with shear deformation and shear areas of about 4.5 in^2 (column) and 5 to 6 in^2 (beam) - values of the size of the web areas of such shapes, NOT taken from any table and NOT entered in the model - the same analysis gives 0.145 in, 5.64 and 6.21 kips. The beam area, which the source does not give either, is not the cause: between 5 in^2 and an axially rigid beam the average drift of grid lines B and C changes by less than 1e-5 in and the base shears by 0.15 %. The second-order to first-order drift ratios of the engine (C.1A LRFD 0.28935 / 0.17770 = 1.63, source 1.60; C.1B LRFD 1.45, source 1.41) are consistent with the same difference in first-order stiffness. Because the shear areas are not given by the source and no opened reference supplies them, the example cannot be completed without a guess: not usable as a target at the printed precision; kept, evaluated and reported, never counted as passed. ASD: the leaning-column load is entered as printed in the source's figure (154 kips; 60.0 ft x 2.56 kip/ft is 153.6 kips).

* limits: The source does not state the beam area, the leaning column / link properties or the treatment of axial and shear deformation in its software; see README.md for the measured influence of the choices made here.
* **quarantine diagnosis**: 4 of 7 targets missed (drift, base shears, and column moments where printed with three figures). Cause: the source's model is not completely specified, and the missing part matters at the printed precision. The source's figures are output of unnamed analysis software; the source gives Ag and Ix of the W12x65 and Ix of the W18x40 but no shear areas, and does not say whether shear deformation is included. The model built here from the given data (no shear deformation) is systematically stiffer: first-order drift 1.8 % to 2.2 % smaller, base shears and column moments 0.4 % to 1.0 % larger than printed, vertical reactions reproduced (they follow from statics). Evidence that shear deformation in the source's software is the cause (parameter study, README.md; Example C.1B, first order, LRFD: printed drift 0.145 in, base shears 5.64 and 6.21 kips; without shear deformation 0.1422 in, 5.671 and 6.247 kips): with shear deformation and shear areas of about 4.5 in^2 (column) and 5 to 6 in^2 (beam) - values of the size of the web areas of such shapes, NOT taken from any table and NOT entered in the model - the same analysis gives 0.145 in, 5.64 and 6.21 kips. The beam area, which the source does not give either, is not the cause: between 5 in^2 and an axially rigid beam the average drift of grid lines B and C changes by less than 1e-5 in and the base shears by 0.15 %. The second-order to first-order drift ratios of the engine (C.1A LRFD 0.28935 / 0.17770 = 1.63, source 1.60; C.1B LRFD 1.45, source 1.41) are consistent with the same difference in first-order stiffness. Because the shear areas are not given by the source and no opened reference supplies them, the example cannot be completed without a guess: not usable as a target at the printed precision; kept, evaluated and reported, never counted as passed.

* **quarantine diagnosis**: 5 of 7 targets missed (drift, base shears, and column moments where printed with three figures). Cause: the source's model is not completely specified, and the missing part matters at the printed precision. The source's figures are output of unnamed analysis software; the source gives Ag and Ix of the W12x65 and Ix of the W18x40 but no shear areas, and does not say whether shear deformation is included. The model built here from the given data (no shear deformation) is systematically stiffer: first-order drift 1.8 % to 2.2 % smaller, base shears and column moments 0.4 % to 1.0 % larger than printed, vertical reactions reproduced (they follow from statics). Evidence that shear deformation in the source's software is the cause (parameter study, README.md; Example C.1B, first order, LRFD: printed drift 0.145 in, base shears 5.64 and 6.21 kips; without shear deformation 0.1422 in, 5.671 and 6.247 kips): with shear deformation and shear areas of about 4.5 in^2 (column) and 5 to 6 in^2 (beam) - values of the size of the web areas of such shapes, NOT taken from any table and NOT entered in the model - the same analysis gives 0.145 in, 5.64 and 6.21 kips. The beam area, which the source does not give either, is not the cause: between 5 in^2 and an axially rigid beam the average drift of grid lines B and C changes by less than 1e-5 in and the base shears by 0.15 %. The second-order to first-order drift ratios of the engine (C.1A LRFD 0.28935 / 0.17770 = 1.63, source 1.60; C.1B LRFD 1.45, source 1.41) are consistent with the same difference in first-order stiffness. Because the shear areas are not given by the source and no opened reference supplies them, the example cannot be completed without a guess: not usable as a target at the printed precision; kept, evaluated and reported, never counted as passed. ASD: the leaning-column load is entered as printed in the source's figure (154 kips; 60.0 ft x 2.56 kip/ft is 153.6 kips).

* tier C (independent solvers): passed — 1 combination(s) cross-checked by OpenSeesPy and PyNite Largest: opensees vs pynite: 1.05e-14 (tol. 1.00e-06); benchfea vs pynite: 8.13e-15 (tol. 1.00e-06).

* recorded: target memberStation(member=s, quantity=Mz, relative=0.5): expected -5997920, engine -6000000 (difference 2.080e+03, tolerance {'rel': 1e-06})
* **quarantine diagnosis**: Missed by 3.5e-4 relative: engine moment -6,000,000 lb in, i.e. a stress of -11,403.95 psi; printed target -11400.000 psi. The page prints a value rounded to three significant figures (evidently the textbook's rounded answer) with three decimals, so the printed-precision rule assigns it the floor tolerance 1e-6, which its real precision (5e-3 x 11,400 ... half a unit of the third figure) does not support. The exact value follows from statics: the moment in the middle portion is w a^2 / 2 = (10000/12) x 120^2 / 2 = 6.0e6 lb in, the stress 6.0e6 x 15 / 7892 = 11,403.95 psi (ODE oracle = engine to 1e-15). The page's deflection target (0.182 in) reproduces and is a target of the active problem.

Cause: rounding in the source's hand calculation - its stiffness coefficients are carried with three or four significant figures and later results are computed from rounded earlier ones, so the last printed digit of its results is not reliable, while the policy counts every printed digit as significant. Evidence: the source's condensed stiffness coefficients carry three or four digits (0.00355, 4.961, 15.257e4); the exact solution of its printed assembled equations is u3 = 11.3665 mm, which the source prints truncated as 11.36. The differences are not caused by the area used here for 'axial deformation neglected': the engine's values change by less than 1e-6 relative between areas of 1e8 and 1e9 mm^2. The engine's results for this model are confirmed by the other tiers that apply (tier C: OpenSeesPy and PyNite agree with the engine; tier D: equilibrium and the other invariants pass); the difference is between the source's printed digits and the exact solution of the stated problem.

* **quarantine diagnosis**: The engine reproduces the printed displacements and the moment-diagram value at b (17.68 kN m; statics: 5/sqrt 2 kN x 5 m = 17.678), but 3 of the 4 printed reactions are missed. Engine: R_xa = -3.5355 kN, R_ya = -3.3146 kN, R_mza = -8.8388 kN m, R_yb = 6.8501 kN. Printed: -3.60, -3.30, -8.80, 6.85. Cause: rounding in the source's hand calculation - its stiffness coefficients are carried with three or four significant figures and later results are computed from rounded earlier ones, so the last printed digit of its results is not reliable, while the policy counts every printed digit as significant. Evidence: the printed reactions do not satisfy equilibrium with the stated load - horizontal equilibrium requires R_xa = -5/sqrt 2 = -3.536 kN (printed -3.60, 1.8 % off), and moments about b require R_mza = 8 m x R_ya + 17.678 kN m, which the printed pair (-3.30, -8.80) misses by 0.08 kN m. The source obtains them by multiplying stiffness coefficients with displacements rounded to two or three significant figures (200 x 0.750 x 0.024 = 3.60). The engine's results for this model are confirmed by the other tiers that apply (tier C: OpenSeesPy and PyNite agree with the engine; tier D: equilibrium and the other invariants pass); the difference is between the source's printed digits and the exact solution of the stated problem.

* **quarantine diagnosis**: Source value is a discretised approximation; no stated error bound. Engine (exact stability functions): 6609.41 kN; printed 6630 kN (one element per member with the polynomial geometric stiffness of the source's Eq. 9.14); difference 0.31 %, tolerance from the printed precision 7.5e-5. Evidence that the difference is the source's discretisation error: a plane-frame calculation with the source's own element matrices (Eq. 4.34 and Eq. 9.14) gives 6629.6 kN with one element per member - the printed value - and 6610.8 and 6609.39 kN with two and eight elements per member, converging to the engine's value. (That calculation is a diagnosis aid, not a verification tier.) Buckling factors cannot be cross-checked by the foreign solvers of tier C.

* **quarantine diagnosis**: All three targets missed by 3 % to 4.5 %: engine -971.71, 908.02, 280.31 kN m; figure labels of the source 932, 869, 272 kN m. The source's values come from an incremental analysis with one element per member whose solution scheme and step size are not stated; no error bound is given. Evidence that the difference is not the source's discretisation: the source's own element matrices (Eq. 4.34 with the geometric stiffness of Eq. 9.14, one element per member), solved here to equilibrium with the member axial forces iterated, give 971.5, 907.8 and 280.3 kN m - the engine's values within 0.03 % - so the difference of 4 % lies in the source's solution process (an incremental scheme without equilibrium iteration underestimates the displacements of a softening structure; the source discusses this drift in its Section 12.2) or in its geometry updating, which cannot be determined from the example. The other tiers that apply pass on this model (tier C: largest difference to OpenSeesPy 1.5e-4 against an allowed 4.4e-4, to PyNite 2.1e-5 against 3.2e-5; tiers D and E); the problem is quarantined for its published targets only. Read off a figure; approximate method in the source.

The source's values come from an incremental analysis (two elements per column) whose solution scheme and step size are not stated; no error bound is given. The first-order moments of the same example (316, 8708, 2044) and its critical load factor (2.20) are reproduced (problems ex-9-6-portal-first-order and ex-9-6-portal-critical-load). The other tiers that apply pass on this model (tier C: largest difference to OpenSeesPy 1.9e-4 against an allowed 5.6e-4, to PyNite 1.9e-5 against 3.8e-5; tiers D and E); the problem is quarantined for its published targets only. Read off a figure; approximate method in the source.

* **quarantine diagnosis**: Source value is a discretised approximation; no stated error bound. Engine (exact stability functions): 214.05 kips = pi^2 EI/L^2 with L = 156 in (buckling in two half waves, as the source's text states for a sufficiently stiff brace); label on the source's plot 215.7 kips (four elements with the polynomial geometric stiffness of Eq. 9.14); difference 0.77 %, tolerance from the printed precision 2.3e-4. Evidence: a plane-frame calculation with the source's own element matrices gives 215.66 kips with four elements - the printed value - and 214.06 kips with sixteen. Buckling factors cannot be cross-checked by the foreign solvers of tier C.

* tier C (independent solvers): passed — 2 combination(s) cross-checked by OpenSeesPy and PyNite Largest: benchfea vs opensees: 3.16e-04 (tol. 9.47e-04); opensees vs pynite: 3.14e-04 (tol. 9.77e-04).

* recorded: target memberStation(member=m, quantity=Mz, relative=0.0, combination=second): expected 61.37, engine 61.37995502 (difference 9.955e-03, tolerance {'rel': 8.147303242626691e-05})
* recorded: target nodeDisplacement(node=b, dof=ux, combination=second): expected 1.145, engine 1.145998501 (difference 9.985e-04, tolerance {'rel': 0.0004366812227074236})
* **quarantine diagnosis**: Both values missed by 2.0 times the tolerance: moment engine 61.380 kip in, printed 61.37; deflection engine 1.1460 in, printed 1.145. Cause: rounding in the page's hand evaluation of the closed form. The page carries alpha = 1.226, tan(alpha)/alpha = 2.273 and M0 = 2.25 ft kip and prints '5.115 ft k = 61.37 in k' although 5.115 x 12 = 61.38; for the deflection it multiplies 0.451 by 2.537. In double precision the same closed forms give alpha = 1.22642, M = 0.09 x 300 x 2.273332 = 61.380 kip in and delta = 0.451228 x 2.539731 = 1.14600 in, the ODE oracle's and the engine's values. The program results printed on the same page are 61.36 and 1.14538.

* recorded: tier D: 23 invariant check(s) over 2 combination(s) and the statics of 66 result station(s): FAILED: station statics: LC1: member m1: station forces at the ends differ from the end forces by 5.88e-04; station statics: LC1: member m2: station forces at the ends differ from the end forces by 9.18e-04; station statics: LC1: member m3: station forces at the ends differ from the end forces by 1.15e-03; station statics: LC2: member m1: station forces at the ends differ from the end forces by 4.38e-03
* recorded: tier E: 2 check(s) applied (designVariables, memberSplit); NOT passed: memberSplit (difference above tolerance)
* **quarantine diagnosis**: The engine produces no results: it refuses both combinations with 'invariant failed' (global force equilibrium 4.0e-1, global moment equilibrium 3.2e-1, joint equilibrium 3.9e-1 against the tolerance 1e-6). Cause: a limitation of the engine's invariant check, not an error of its solution. The frame is statically determinate, so a support settlement produces a rigid-body motion with NO member forces and NO reactions; the invariants measure the equilibrium residual relative to the largest force present, and here every force is round-off of zero, so the relative residual is of order one. The reference solution (plane frame program of verification/derive/vendor_reference.py) gives the rigid-body displacements the page prints (1.25 cm, 0.001667 rad, 0.4167 cm) with forces of 1e-12 kN. The engine's displacements cannot be read because it returns no results. Reported as an engine limitation (README.md); nothing was changed to make the problem run (for instance by adding a load).

* **quarantine diagnosis**: Missed by 2.3e-5 relative (13 times the tolerance): engine -2755.035 lb in, printed 2,754.97. The page's value comes from a closed-form expression that neglects axial deformation, evaluated on the page with rounded section properties (it prints I = 10.67 and 1.333 in^4 and '= 2,754 in-lb' next to the formula, 2,754.97 in its table). The same expression with the exact properties (I_b / I_c = 8) gives 2755.10 lb in; the frame of the statement, with axial deformation, gives 2755.03 lb in (reference program of verification/derive/vendor_reference.py = engine to 1e-9). The printed digits beyond the fourth are not significant. The page's other two values (500 and 27.55 lb) reproduce and are targets of the active problem.

* recorded: target bucklingFactor(combination=C, mode=1): expected 7.857, engine 7.85626094 (difference 7.391e-04, tolerance {'rel': 6.363752068219422e-05})
* **quarantine diagnosis**: Cause: discretisation of the data set. Its ratio comes from an eigenvalue analysis with 4 elements per member and a polynomial geometric stiffness, which overestimates the critical load; the engine's members carry the exact stability functions. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py): a plane-frame program written here with the data set's own elements (cubic elements, consistent geometric stiffness, axial forces of a linear analysis, perfect geometry, 0.8 E) gives 7.856995, the data set's stored value 7.857009 within 2e-06; the engine gives 7.856261, lower by 9.5e-05 of the value, which is more than half a unit of the third decimal the data set displays (7.857 against the engine's 7.856).

* limits: The member numbering of the data set is not defined in its files; the identification of the members was inferred from the first-order values (README.md), so these targets are not independent evidence of the identification.
* **quarantine diagnosis**: One of three targets missed by 1.24 times the tolerance: member 2 (right column) engine 2131.4762 kip in, displayed 2131.47 (stored 2131.47251). Cause: precision of the input listing, not of the analysis. The data set's model file lists loads (and coordinates) with five significant figures, while its results were computed with the unrounded model and are displayed to 0.01 kip in, i.e. to 1e-6 ... 1e-5 of the moments; a load rounded in the fifth figure changes a moment by up to 5e-5 of its value. Evidence: the first-order joint displacements of the same model, displayed with three decimals, are reproduced; every first-order result of this frame (displacements and moments, both cases) exceeds the stored value by the same factor 1 + 1.73e-6, as a load factor would: the loads listed as 34.849 and 348.49 kip are not exactly the loads of the analysis.

* recorded: target nodeDisplacement(node=n9, dof=ux): expected -1.188, engine -1.191179095 (difference 3.179e-03, tolerance {'rel': 0.0004208754208754209})
* recorded: target nodeDisplacement(node=n13, dof=ux): expected -1.163, engine -1.165030497 (difference 2.030e-03, tolerance {'rel': 0.0004299226139294927})
* recorded: target memberExtreme(member=m1-4, quantity=Mz, kind=absmax): expected 2421.8, engine 2422.884144 (difference 1.084e+00, tolerance {'rel': 2.0645800644148977e-06})
* recorded: target memberExtreme(member=m9-12, quantity=Mz, kind=absmax): expected 2380.57, engine 2380.219093 (difference 3.509e-01, tolerance {'rel': 2.100337314172656e-06})
* recorded: target memberExtreme(member=m5-8, quantity=Mz, kind=absmax): expected 1275.17, engine 1277.280951 (difference 2.111e+00, tolerance {'rel': 3.921045821341468e-06})
* **quarantine diagnosis**: All 5 targets missed: displacements by 2.7e-3 and 1.8e-3 relative, moments by 1.5e-4 ... 1.7e-3. Cause: different theory. The data set's WC values come from MASTAN2's incremental work-control analysis (step 0.001) in an updated Lagrangian formulation, 4 elements per member: a large-displacement analysis. The engine solves the classical small-displacement second-order theory (P-Delta and P-delta with exact stability functions; docs/THEORY.md 10 and 12), which neglects terms of second order in the kinematics such as the shortening of the chord of a bent member. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py, case 'Right', joint displacements): OpenSeesPy with the linearised 'PDelta' transformation, 32 elements per member, agrees with the engine within 4.8e-5; OpenSeesPy with the 'Corotational' (large-displacement) transformation agrees with the data set within 1.4e-3 (32 elements per member; 1.6e-3 with the data set's own 4 elements per member), while the engine differs from the data set by up to 3.5e-3. The remaining difference between the corotational results and the data set (element formulation, increment size) was not resolved. The targets are outside what the engine's theory can reproduce at the printed precision; they are kept, evaluated and reported, and never counted as passed.

* tier D (invariants): passed — 12 invariant check(s) over 1 combination(s) and the statics of 30 result station(s): all passed Largest: reciprocity (Maxwell-Betti): 1.13e-13 (tol. 1.00e-09); station statics (end, member m5-8 station forces at the ends against the end forces): 6.11e-16 (tol. 1.00e-09).

* limits: The member numbering of the data set is not defined in its files; the identification of the members was inferred from the first-order values (README.md), so these targets are not independent evidence of the identification.
* **quarantine diagnosis**: 2 of 4 targets missed, by up to 1.5e-5 relative (engine 778.888 and 3948.680 kip in, displayed 778.90 and 3948.69; tolerance 0.005 kip in). Cause: precision of the input listing, not of the analysis. The data set's model file lists loads (and coordinates) with five significant figures, while its results were computed with the unrounded model and are displayed to 0.01 kip in, i.e. to 1e-6 ... 1e-5 of the moments; a load rounded in the fifth figure changes a moment by up to 5e-5 of its value. Evidence: the first-order joint displacements of the same model, displayed with three decimals, are reproduced; the differences of the four moments are -1.5e-5 ... -1.7e-6, not a common factor (nine listed loads, each rounded separately).

* recorded: target memberExtreme(member=m13-16, quantity=Mz, kind=absmax): expected 4500.83, engine 4490.867571 (difference 9.962e+00, tolerance {'rel': 1.1109062106322613e-06})
* **quarantine diagnosis**: All 7 targets missed: displacements by 2.2e-3 ... 2.1e-2 relative, moments by 2.2e-3 ... 1.3e-2. Cause: different theory. The data set's WC values come from MASTAN2's incremental work-control analysis (step 0.001) in an updated Lagrangian formulation, 4 elements per member: a large-displacement analysis. The engine solves the classical small-displacement second-order theory (P-Delta and P-delta with exact stability functions; docs/THEORY.md 10 and 12), which neglects terms of second order in the kinematics such as the shortening of the chord of a bent member. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py, case 'Right', joint displacements): OpenSeesPy with the linearised 'PDelta' transformation, 32 elements per member, agrees with the engine within 3.0e-5; OpenSeesPy with the 'Corotational' (large-displacement) transformation agrees with the data set within 1.5e-3 (32 elements per member; 1.2e-3 with the data set's own 4 elements per member), while the engine differs from the data set by up to 1.8e-2. The remaining difference between the corotational results and the data set (element formulation, increment size) was not resolved. The targets are outside what the engine's theory can reproduce at the printed precision; they are kept, evaluated and reported, and never counted as passed.

Cause NOT identified. What was established: the stiffness model is the data set's (the critical load ratio of the same model is reproduced to 6e-5, and to 6e-6 by the cubic-element program of verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py relative to the engine); the differences alternate in sign between the left and the right column line, the pattern of a different distribution of the loads between the two column lines (the lateral displacements of this frame depend on the difference of the two gravity loads, listed as 235.25 and 239.88 kip, through the diagonals), not of a stiffness difference. Variants tried without success: E = 29,000 ksi for the pin-ended members, the two pin-ended sections exchanged. Open question for the data set, see README.md.

* recorded: target nodeDisplacement(node=n9, dof=ux): expected -1.902, engine -1.923588437 (difference 2.159e-02, tolerance {'rel': 0.0002628811777076761})
* recorded: target nodeDisplacement(node=n10, dof=ux): expected -1.862, engine -1.88403085 (difference 2.203e-02, tolerance {'rel': 0.0002685284640171858})
* recorded: target nodeDisplacement(node=n17, dof=ux): expected -1.926, engine -1.94613809 (difference 2.014e-02, tolerance {'rel': 0.00025960539979231567})
* recorded: target nodeDisplacement(node=n18, dof=ux): expected -1.885, engine -1.906705458 (difference 2.171e-02, tolerance {'rel': 0.00026525198938992045})
* **quarantine diagnosis**: All 4 targets missed by 1.05e-2 ... 1.2e-2 relative. Cause: different theory. The data set's WC values come from MASTAN2's incremental work-control analysis (step 0.001) in an updated Lagrangian formulation, 4 elements per member: a large-displacement analysis. The engine solves the classical small-displacement second-order theory (P-Delta and P-delta with exact stability functions; docs/THEORY.md 10 and 12), which neglects terms of second order in the kinematics such as the shortening of the chord of a bent member. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py, case 'Right', joint displacements): OpenSeesPy with the linearised 'PDelta' transformation, 32 elements per member, agrees with the engine within 3.0e-4; OpenSeesPy with the 'Corotational' (large-displacement) transformation agrees with the data set within 6.6e-3 (32 elements per member; 1.3e-2 with the data set's own 4 elements per member), while the engine differs from the data set by up to 1.2e-2. The remaining difference between the corotational results and the data set (element formulation, increment size) was not resolved. The targets are outside what the engine's theory can reproduce at the printed precision; they are kept, evaluated and reported, and never counted as passed. In addition the first-order displacements of this frame are not reproduced (frame-3-first-order-displacements-left).

Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py, case 'Right', joint displacements): OpenSeesPy with the linearised 'PDelta' transformation, 32 elements per member, agrees with the engine within 3.8e-5; OpenSeesPy with the 'Corotational' (large-displacement) transformation agrees with the data set within 2.1e-3 (32 elements per member; 1.3e-3 with the data set's own 4 elements per member), while the engine differs from the data set by up to 1.6e-2. The remaining difference between the corotational results and the data set (element formulation, increment size) was not resolved. The targets are outside what the engine's theory can reproduce at the printed precision; they are kept, evaluated and reported, and never counted as passed.

* **quarantine diagnosis**: Cause: discretisation of the data set. Its ratio comes from an eigenvalue analysis with 4 elements per member and a polynomial geometric stiffness, which overestimates the critical load; the engine's members carry the exact stability functions. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py): a plane-frame program written here with the data set's own elements (cubic elements, consistent geometric stiffness, axial forces of a linear analysis, perfect geometry, 0.8 E) gives 6.353614, the data set's stored value 6.353606 within 1e-06; the engine gives 6.353220, lower by 6.1e-05 of the value, which is more than half a unit of the third decimal the data set displays (6.354 against the engine's 6.353).

#### `ziemian/frame-7-second-order-right` — Ziemian frame 7 (single-storey two-bay frame with a diagonal brace and a lean-on column): second-order joint displacements, case 'right'

Outcome: **quarantined** (declared `quarantined`). Source: C. W. Ziemian, R. D. Ziemian, Steel benchmark frames for structural analysis and validation studies: Finite element models and numerical simulation data, Data in Brief 39 (2021) 107564, doi:10.1016/j.dib.2021.107564, with the data set on Mendeley Data, doi:10.17632/39sjhchwtx.1; edition: data set version 1 (2021), licence CC BY 4.0; location: Frame 7: 'FEA Model Details_All frames.xlsx', tab 'Frame 7'; 'Simulation Results_All frames.xlsx', tab 'Frame 7', columns WC / Right; pages: article Tables 2 and 3 (modelling and analysis conditions); 'Description Of Frames.pdf'. Basis: published.

* recorded: the engine produced no results for model.json: combination 'C': the load exceeds the elastic critical load (the tangent stiffness K + Kg of the structure is not positive definite); buckling factor ~ 0.851 (the largest multiple of the first-order axial forces at which the stiffness is still positive definite; a value below 1 means the loads are beyond the critical level)
* **quarantine diagnosis**: The engine produces no results: it refuses the combination as beyond the elastic critical load (buckling factor about 0.85). Cause: in the case 'Right' the only diagonal brace (one pin-ended element, W4x13, 375 in long) is in compression: first-order force 21.68 kip against its Euler load pi^2 (0.8 E) I / L^2 = 18.45 kip (ratio 1.18; verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py). The engine's pin-ended member is a beam-column that buckles at its Euler load; the data set's single pin-ended element has no member buckling (the data set reports displacements 2.340 and 2.342 in for this case). The description file of the data set states that only the tension brace is modelled, i.e. the frame is meant for the case 'Left'. Not usable as a target for this engine.

* **quarantine diagnosis**: All 16 targets missed: displacements by 4e-3 ... 1.4e-2 relative, moments by up to 2.6e-3. Gravity loads only, see frame-9-second-order-right. Cause: different theory. The data set's WC values come from MASTAN2's incremental work-control analysis (step 0.001) in an updated Lagrangian formulation, 4 elements per member: a large-displacement analysis. The engine solves the classical small-displacement second-order theory (P-Delta and P-delta with exact stability functions; docs/THEORY.md 10 and 12), which neglects terms of second order in the kinematics such as the shortening of the chord of a bent member. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py, case 'Right', joint displacements): OpenSeesPy with the linearised 'PDelta' transformation, 32 elements per member, agrees with the engine within 1.2e-4; OpenSeesPy with the 'Corotational' (large-displacement) transformation agrees with the data set within 4.3e-3 (32 elements per member; 4.3e-3 with the data set's own 4 elements per member), while the engine differs from the data set by up to 2.7e-2. The remaining difference between the corotational results and the data set (element formulation, increment size) was not resolved. The targets are outside what the engine's theory can reproduce at the printed precision; they are kept, evaluated and reported, and never counted as passed.

* **quarantine diagnosis**: All 16 targets missed: displacements by 8e-3 ... 2.8e-2 relative (e.g. joint 13: engine -0.43277 in, data set -0.421399), moments by up to 1.1e-2. This frame carries gravity loads only; its small sway (0.4 ... 0.8 in) is sensitive to the horizontal movements caused by the shortening of the chords of the heavily loaded beams. Cause: different theory. The data set's WC values come from MASTAN2's incremental work-control analysis (step 0.001) in an updated Lagrangian formulation, 4 elements per member: a large-displacement analysis. The engine solves the classical small-displacement second-order theory (P-Delta and P-delta with exact stability functions; docs/THEORY.md 10 and 12), which neglects terms of second order in the kinematics such as the shortening of the chord of a bent member. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py, case 'Right', joint displacements): OpenSeesPy with the linearised 'PDelta' transformation, 32 elements per member, agrees with the engine within 1.2e-4; OpenSeesPy with the 'Corotational' (large-displacement) transformation agrees with the data set within 4.3e-3 (32 elements per member; 4.3e-3 with the data set's own 4 elements per member), while the engine differs from the data set by up to 2.7e-2. The remaining difference between the corotational results and the data set (element formulation, increment size) was not resolved. The targets are outside what the engine's theory can reproduce at the printed precision; they are kept, evaluated and reported, and never counted as passed.

## 3. Quarantined, failed and invalid cases

* **`aisc-de/c1a-first-order-asd`** — quarantined. 5 of 7 targets missed (drift, base shears, and column moments where printed with three figures). Cause: the source's model is not completely specified, and the missing part matters at the printed precision. The source's figures are output of unnamed analysis software; the source gives Ag and Ix of the W12x65 and Ix of the W18x40 but no shear areas, and does not say whether shear deformation is included. The model built here from the given data (no shear deformation) is systematically stiffer: first-order drift 1.8 % to 2.2 % smaller, base shears and column moments 0.4 % to 1.0 % larger than printed, vertical reactions reproduced (they follow from statics). Evidence that shear deformation in the source's software is the cause (parameter study, README.md; Example C.1B, first order, LRFD: printed drift 0.145 in, base shears 5.64 and 6.21 kips; without shear deformation 0.1422 in, 5.671 and 6.247 kips): with shear deformation and shear areas of about 4.5 in^2 (column) and 5 to 6 in^2 (beam) - values of the size of the web areas of such shapes, NOT taken from any table and NOT entered in the model - the same analysis gives 0.145 in, 5.64 and 6.21 kips. The beam area, which the source does not give either, is not the cause: between 5 in^2 and an axially rigid beam the average drift of grid lines B and C changes by less than 1e-5 in and the base shears by 0.15 %. The second-order to first-order drift ratios of the engine (C.1A LRFD 0.28935 / 0.17770 = 1.63, source 1.60; C.1B LRFD 1.45, source 1.41) are consistent with the same difference in first-order stiffness. Because the shear areas are not given by the source and no opened reference supplies them, the example cannot be completed without a guess: not usable as a target at the printed precision; kept, evaluated and reported, never counted as passed. ASD: the leaning-column load is entered as printed in the source's figure (154 kips; 60.0 ft x 2.56 kip/ft is 153.6 kips). Findings: target nodeDisplacement(node=B1, dof=ux): expected 0.193, engine 0.1894248202 (difference 3.575e-03, tolerance {'rel': 0.0025906735751295338}); target memberStation(member=colB, quantity=Mz, relative=1.0): expected 1443.84, engine 1451.825478 (difference 7.985e+00, tolerance {'rel': 0.0006648936170212766}); target reaction(node=B0, component=Fx): expected 6.016, engine 6.049272823 (difference 3.327e-02, tolerance {'rel': 0.0013297872340425534}); target memberStation(member=colC, quantity=Mz, relative=1.0): expected -1589.76, engine -1599.185478 (difference 9.425e+00, tolerance {'rel': 0.000603864734299517}); target reaction(node=C0, component=Fx): expected -6.624, engine -6.663272823 (difference 3.927e-02, tolerance {'rel': 0.001207729468599034})

The beam area, which the source does not give either, is not the cause: between 5 in^2 and an axially rigid beam the average drift of grid lines B and C changes by less than 1e-5 in and the base shears by 0.15 %. The second-order to first-order drift ratios of the engine (C.1A LRFD 0.28935 / 0.17770 = 1.63, source 1.60; C.1B LRFD 1.45, source 1.41) are consistent with the same difference in first-order stiffness. Because the shear areas are not given by the source and no opened reference supplies them, the example cannot be completed without a guess: not usable as a target at the printed precision; kept, evaluated and reported, never counted as passed. Evidence: the source computes them from the rotation rounded to four digits (200 x 1.0e5 x 0.001786 = 35.72; the unrounded rotation is 1/560 = 0.0017857 rad, giving 35.714). The engine's results for this model are confirmed by the other tiers that apply (tier C: OpenSeesPy and PyNite agree with the engine; tier D: equilibrium and the other invariants pass); the difference is between the source's printed digits and the exact solution of the stated problem. Both values missed by 2.0 times the tolerance: moment engine 61.380 kip in, printed 61.37; deflection engine 1.1460 in, printed 1.145. Cause: rounding in the page's hand evaluation of the closed form. The page carries alpha = 1.226, tan(alpha)/alpha = 2.273 and M0 = 2.25 ft kip and prints '5.115 ft k = 61.37 in k' although 5.115 x 12 = 61.38; for the deflection it multiplies 0.451 by 2.537. In double precision the same closed forms give alpha = 1.22642, M = 0.09 x 300 x 2.273332 = 61.380 kip in and delta = 0.451228 x 2.539731 = 1.14600 in, the ODE oracle's and the engine's values. The program results printed on the same page are 61.36 and 1.14538. Findings: target memberStation(member=m, quantity=Mz, relative=0.0, combination=second): expected 61.37, engine 61.37995502 (difference 9.955e-03, tolerance {'rel': 8.147303242626691e-05}); target nodeDisplacement(node=b, dof=ux, combination=second): expected 1.145, engine 1.145998501 (difference 9.985e-04, tolerance {'rel': 0.0004366812227074236})
* **`staad/cantilever-column-p-delta-vendor-printed`** — quarantined. Missed by 6.4e-4 relative: engine 737.194 kip in, printed 736.72. The page's reference is a one-step approximation, M = H L + P x (FIRST-order tip deflection) = 5 x 144 + 3 x 5.57; the exact second-order solution of the stated beam-column (ODE oracle) is M = 737.194 kip in with a tip deflection of 5.731 in. The approximation omits the growth of the deflection under the axial load (P / P_e = 0.028). The page's first-order tip deflection 5.57 in reproduces and is a target of the active problem. Findings: target memberStation(member=m, quantity=Mz, relative=0.0, combination=second): expected 736.72, engine 737.1942571 (difference 4.743e-01, tolerance {'rel': 6.786838961885112e-06})
* **`staad/frame-support-settlement`** — quarantined. The engine produces no results: it refuses both combinations with 'invariant failed' (global force equilibrium 4.0e-1, global moment equilibrium 3.2e-1, joint equilibrium 3.9e-1 against the tolerance 1e-6). Cause: a limitation of the engine's invariant check, not an error of its solution. The frame is statically determinate, so a support settlement produces a rigid-body motion with NO member forces and NO reactions; the invariants measure the equilibrium residual relative to the largest force present, and here every force is round-off of zero, so the relative residual is of order one. The reference solution (plane frame program of verification/derive/vendor_reference.py) gives the rigid-body displacements the page prints (1.25 cm, 0.001667 rad, 0.4167 cm) with forces of 1e-12 kN. The engine's displacements cannot be read because it returns no results. Reported as an engine limitation (README.md); nothing was changed to make the problem run (for instance by adding a load). Findings: tier C: not passed (LC1: only 1 solver(s) can represent: opensees: failed: self-check failed: constraint violation 0.00e+00, joint equilibrium residual 1.00e+00, converged True; benchfea and pynite differ: endMoment 1.68e+00 at member m2 end i Mz (allowed 1.00e-06); LC2: only 1 solver(s) can represent: opensees: failed: self-check failed: constraint violation 0.00e+00, joint equilibrium residual 1.26e+00, converged True; benchfea and pynite differ: endMoment 1.80e+00 at member m1 end j Mz (allowed 1.00e-06)); tier D: 23 invariant check(s) over 2 combination(s) and the statics of 66 result station(s): FAILED: station statics: LC1: member m1: station forces at the ends differ from the end forces by 5.88e-04; station statics: LC1: member m2: station forces at the ends differ from the end forces by 9.18e-04; station statics: LC1: member m3: station forces at the ends differ from the end forces by 1.15e-03; station statics: LC2: member m1: station forces at the ends differ from the end forces by 4.38e-03; tier E: 2 check(s) applied (designVariables, memberSplit); NOT passed: memberSplit (difference above tolerance)
* **`staad/portal-fixed-uniform-load-vendor-printed`** — quarantined. Missed by 8.1e-4 relative (7 times the tolerance): engine -44.436 kip ft, printed 44.40. The exact value for members without axial deformation is w L^2 / 18 = 44.444 kip ft (reference program with EA x 1e6); with the axial stiffness of the statement 44.436 kip ft (reference program = engine to 1e-9). Findings: target memberStation(member=b, quantity=Mz, relative=0.0): expected -44.4, engine -44.43611423 (difference 3.611e-02, tolerance {'rel': 0.00011261261261261261})

The same expression with the exact properties (I_b / I_c = 8) gives 2755.10 lb in; the frame of the statement, with axial deformation, gives 2755.03 lb in (reference program of verification/derive/vendor_reference.py = engine to 1e-9). The printed digits beyond the fourth are not significant. The page's other two values (500 and 27.55 lb) reproduce and are targets of the active problem. * **`ziemian/frame-1-critical-load-ratio`** — quarantined. Cause: discretisation of the data set. Its ratio comes from an eigenvalue analysis with 4 elements per member and a polynomial geometric stiffness, which overestimates the critical load; the engine's members carry the exact stability functions. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py): a plane-frame program written here with the data set's own elements (cubic elements, consistent geometric stiffness, axial forces of a linear analysis, perfect geometry, 0.8 E) gives 7.856995, the data set's stored value 7.857009 within 2e-06; the engine gives 7.856261, lower by 9.5e-05 of the value, which is more than half a unit of the third decimal the data set displays (7.857 against the engine's 7.856). Findings: target bucklingFactor(combination=C, mode=1): expected 7.857, engine 7.85626094 (difference 7.391e-04, tolerance {'rel': 6.363752068219422e-05})
* **`ziemian/frame-1-first-order-moments-right`** — quarantined. One of three targets missed by 1.24 times the tolerance: member 2 (right column) engine 2131.4762 kip in, displayed 2131.47 (stored 2131.47251). Cause: precision of the input listing, not of the analysis. The data set's model file lists loads (and coordinates) with five significant figures, while its results were computed with the unrounded model and are displayed to 0.01 kip in, i.e. to 1e-6 ... 1e-5 of the moments; a load rounded in the fifth figure changes a moment by up to 5e-5 of its value. Evidence: the first-order joint displacements of the same model, displayed with three decimals, are reproduced; every first-order result of this frame (displacements and moments, both cases) exceeds the stored value by the same factor 1 + 1.73e-6, as a load factor would: the loads listed as 34.849 and 348.49 kip are not exactly the loads of the analysis. Findings: target memberExtreme(member=m9-12, quantity=Mz, kind=absmax): expected 2131.47, engine 2131.476192 (difference 6.192e-03, tolerance {'rel': 2.345798908734348e-06})
* **`ziemian/frame-1-second-order-left`** — quarantined. All 5 targets missed: displacements by 2.7e-3 and 1.8e-3 relative, moments by 1.5e-4 ... 1.7e-3. Cause: different theory. The data set's WC values come from MASTAN2's incremental work-control analysis (step 0.001) in an updated Lagrangian formulation, 4 elements per member: a large-displacement analysis. The engine solves the classical small-displacement second-order theory (P-Delta and P-delta with exact stability functions; docs/THEORY.md 10 and 12), which neglects terms of second order in the kinematics such as the shortening of the chord of a bent member. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py, case 'Right', joint displacements): OpenSeesPy with the linearised 'PDelta' transformation, 32 elements per member, agrees with the engine within 4.8e-5; OpenSeesPy with the 'Corotational' (large-displacement) transformation agrees with the data set within 1.4e-3 (32 elements per member; 1.6e-3 with the data set's own 4 elements per member), while the engine differs from the data set by up to 3.5e-3. The remaining difference between the corotational results and the data set (element formulation, increment size) was not resolved. The targets are outside what the engine's theory can reproduce at the printed precision; they are kept, evaluated and reported, and never counted as passed. Findings: target nodeDisplacement(node=n9, dof=ux): expected -1.188, engine -1.191179095 (difference 3.179e-03, tolerance {'rel': 0.0004208754208754209}); target nodeDisplacement(node=n13, dof=ux): expected -1.163, engine -1.165030497 (difference 2.030e-03, tolerance {'rel': 0.0004299226139294927}); target memberExtreme(member=m1-4, quantity=Mz, kind=absmax): expected 2421.8, engine 2422.884144 (difference 1.084e+00, tolerance {'rel': 2.0645800644148977e-06}); target memberExtreme(member=m9-12, quantity=Mz, kind=absmax): expected 2380.57, engine 2380.219093 (difference 3.509e-01, tolerance {'rel': 2.100337314172656e-06}); target memberExtreme(member=m5-8, quantity=Mz, kind=absmax): expected 1275.17, engine 1277.280951 (difference 2.111e+00, tolerance {'rel': 3.921045821341468e-06})

* **`ziemian/frame-5-second-order-right`** — quarantined. All 4 targets missed by 2.2e-3 ... 1.6e-2 relative (largest: joint 25, engine 2.1776 in, data set 2.2139). Cause: different theory. The data set's WC values come from MASTAN2's incremental work-control analysis (step 0.001) in an updated Lagrangian formulation, 4 elements per member: a large-displacement analysis. The engine solves the classical small-displacement second-order theory (P-Delta and P-delta with exact stability functions; docs/THEORY.md 10 and 12), which neglects terms of second order in the kinematics such as the shortening of the chord of a bent member. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py, case 'Right', joint displacements): OpenSeesPy with the linearised 'PDelta' transformation, 32 elements per member, agrees with the engine within 3.8e-5; OpenSeesPy with the 'Corotational' (large-displacement) transformation agrees with the data set within 2.1e-3 (32 elements per member; 1.3e-3 with the data set's own 4 elements per member), while the engine differs from the data set by up to 1.6e-2. The remaining difference between the corotational results and the data set (element formulation, increment size) was not resolved. The targets are outside what the engine's theory can reproduce at the printed precision; they are kept, evaluated and reported, and never counted as passed. * **`ziemian/frame-7-critical-load-ratio`** — quarantined. Cause: discretisation of the data set. Its ratio comes from an eigenvalue analysis with 4 elements per member and a polynomial geometric stiffness, which overestimates the critical load; the engine's members carry the exact stability functions. Evidence (verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py): a plane-frame program written here with the data set's own elements (cubic elements, consistent geometric stiffness, axial forces of a linear analysis, perfect geometry, 0.8 E) gives 6.353614, the data set's stored value 6.353606 within 1e-06; the engine gives 6.353220, lower by 6.1e-05 of the value, which is more than half a unit of the third decimal the data set displays (6.354 against the engine's 6.353). Findings: target bucklingFactor(combination=C, mode=1): expected 6.354, engine 6.353220472 (difference 7.795e-04, tolerance {'rel': 7.869058860560277e-05})

* **`ziemian/frame-7-second-order-right`** — quarantined. The engine produces no results: it refuses the combination as beyond the elastic critical load (buckling factor about 0.85). Cause: in the case 'Right' the only diagonal brace (one pin-ended element, W4x13, 375 in long) is in compression: first-order force 21.68 kip against its Euler load pi^2 (0.8 E) I / L^2 = 18.45 kip (ratio 1.18; verification/derive/ziemian_diagnosis.py). The engine's pin-ended member is a beam-column that buckles at its Euler load; the data set's single pin-ended element has no member buckling (the data set reports displacements 2.340 and 2.342 in for this case). The description file of the data set states that only the tension brace is modelled, i.e. the frame is meant for the case 'Left'. Not usable as a target for this engine. Findings: the engine produced no results for model.json: combination 'C': the load exceeds the elastic critical load (the tangent stiffness K + Kg of the structure is not positive definite); buckling factor ~ 0.851 (the largest multiple of the first-order axial forces at which the stiffness is still positive definite; a value below 1 means the loads are beyond the critical level)
* **`ziemian/frame-9-first-order-displacements-right`** — quarantined. One of six targets missed by 1.002 times the tolerance, at a rounding boundary: joint 21, stored value -0.2375013 in, displayed -0.238; engine -0.2374990 in. The engine differs from the stored value by 2.3e-6 in (9.5e-6 relative, the size of the rounding of the listed loads, see frame-9-first-order-moments-right), which moves it to the other side of the rounding boundary -0.2375 of the displayed number; the policy compares with the number as displayed. The other five displacements are reproduced. Findings: target nodeDisplacement(node=n21, dof=ux): expected -0.238, engine -0.2374990209 (difference 5.010e-04, tolerance {'rel': 0.002100840336134454})
* **`ziemian/frame-9-first-order-moments-left`** — quarantined. 8 of 10 targets missed, by up to 2.5e-5 relative (largest: member 3, engine 3037.076 kip in, displayed 3037.15; tolerance 0.005 kip in). Cause: precision of the input listing, not of the analysis. The data set's model file lists loads (and coordinates) with five significant figures, while its results were computed with the unrounded model and are displayed to 0.01 kip in, i.e. to 1e-6 ... 1e-5 of the moments; a load rounded in the fifth figure changes a moment by up to 5e-5 of its value. ## 5. Tier F: coverage of the design limit states

Registry `src/benchfea/verify/design_coverage.json`, generated from the design tests by `tools/build_design_coverage.py`. (b) = AISC, Companion to the AISC Steel Construction Manual, Volume 1: Design Examples, Version 16.0 (2023), accompanying ANSI/AISC 360-22. An item is *covered* when it has (a) a hand-calculation test and (b) a test in which a printed strength (or measured value / limit) of a check citing the item is reproduced; *partial* = one of the two is missing or only an intermediate value was reproduced. A design reference that uses an item that is not covered is not publishable as verified.

## 6. Tolerances in force

```json
{
 "A": {
  "relative": 1e-06,
```
